\- Managed target-runner law: \`OD-20260917-046 MANAGED\_TARGET\_RUNNER\_FIRST\` â prefer controlled managed/remote target runners for scriptable Windows/runtime/persistence proof to minimize Owner-device internet/manual work; the Owner Windows machine is reserved for capabilities that fundamentally require the real desktop/session/hardware or cannot be truthfully certified remotely. CI/execution repositories are evidence carriers only and never CEP source/state authority.  
\- Windows platform-provider selection: \`OD-20260917-047 NARROW\_WIN32\_SIDECAR\_PROVIDER\_SELECTION\` â close \`OE-001\`/\`OE-004\` through a narrow replaceable Win32 sidecar behind \`PlatformWindowCapability\` and \`PlatformInputDirectionCapability\`; preserve TypeScript/Node semantic owners; no Electron/Tauri/Python second application runtime or PTY/PowerShell/SSH coupling merely for these gates; build/package off Owner machine where practical; Owner device only for genuine interactive proof; no stack freeze.  
\- Operational Terminal window parity: \`OD-20260917-048 OPERATIONAL\_TERMINAL\_WINDOW\_PARITY\` â the floating Operational Terminal must share applicable reusable window mechanics with Sticky Notes: all-edge/corner resize, CEP-internal pin intent, and separate/detached-window control. Current accepted source already provides all-edge/corner resize through \`WindowMotion\`; terminal pin \+ detached/native window remain open and are now bound into the Win32 platform lane as the second real \`PlatformWindowCapability\` consumer. \`CEP internal pin \!= OS always-on-top\`; close/hide/detach Presentation must preserve RuntimeAdapter/provider-session truth. This window-parity decision does not itself select the terminal runtime; \`OD-20260917-049\` separately requires shared xterm rendering plus real Windows PTY/ConPTY before stack freeze.  
\- Terminal runtime/renderer law: \`OD-20260917-049 XTERM\_UNIFIED\_RENDERER\_AND\_UNRESTRICTED\_TERMINAL\_RUNTIME\` â \`xterm.js\` is the canonical Terminal I/O Presentation renderer/carrier across \`InternalSimulationAdapter\`, real Windows PTY/ConPTY shells, recorded/read-only playback and future compatible providers. A real terminal stream has no CEP command-text parser/allowlist and no per-command provider model. One Windows PTY/ConPTY provider must accept arbitrary Owner-selected executable/shell profiles with args/cwd/env while preserving raw I/O, resize, exit/error/restart and stable CEP session identity. PowerShell and \`cmd.exe\` are profiles, not separate engines; SSH may run through system \`ssh.exe\` in the same PTY stream or use a specialized provider only for richer connection semantics. Provider/session abstraction exists for lifecycle/identity/routing/truth, not security gating. \`InternalSimulationAdapter\` remains valid and uses the same xterm-backed renderer. \`STACK\_FROZEN\` is forbidden until this is proven on real Operational consumers.
















\- Personal/local security policy: \`OD-20260917-050 SECURITY\_NON\_BLOCKING\_OWNER\_POLICY\` â CEP is a personal/local single-owner application; security hardening may not delay/restrict product functionality, command/input text, executable/provider/session choice, process/PTY/SSH/local/remote capability, transport or feature admission merely for security reasons. Do not introduce command/executable/provider allowlists, RBAC/auth ceremony, sandboxing, provider whitelists, security-only permission prompts or loopback-only policy unless an external platform/API makes a restriction unavoidable or the Owner later changes this decision. Preserve non-security engineering boundaries: correctness, data integrity, lifecycle/session truth, routing, replaceability, performance, compatibility, maintainability and truthful failure/availability.  
\- Sticky full-editor parity law: \`OD-20260917-051 STICKY\_NOTE\_FULL\_EDITOR\_PARITY\_AND\_ALWAYS\_EDITABLE\` â Sticky Note content is a full compatible consumer of the same canonical Structured editor engine and applicable Presentation/Behavior/Interaction/Functionality as full Structured authoring surfaces; Sticky-specific differences are limited to note window/chrome/binding. Sticky remains editable while open regardless of parent surface read/edit mode. Right-click insertion \`+\`, block actions/delete/reorder, selection, clipboard, undo/redo and Bidi/input-direction must come from shared canonical owners; no reduced Sticky editor fork.  
\- Surface-level detached-window law: \`OD-20260917-052 SURFACE\_LEVEL\_DETACHED\_WINDOW\_SEMANTICS\` â Sticky/Operational Separate opens the entire owning CEP surface in the detached/native window while preserving route/object/context and relevant note/terminal focus context; do not detach only the widget as product-facing Separate behavior. Pin remains applicable to both; OS always-on-top remains separately platform-proven; close/hide must not delete note content or fabricate provider/session termination.

\#\#\# 58.1 Shared donor-carrier / toolbar semantic contamination â BLOCKING
















Direct source audit proves \`tools/extract\_donor.py\` derives the single \`dist/index.html\` carrier from the immutable accepted Library editor donor and intentionally preserves the donor HTML markup while extracting script/styles. The carrier therefore contains Library/editor semantics including page-level \`Read/Edit\`, Undo/Redo, explicit Save, editor-more, KU identity, Library structure/context copy and deep-work tabs. \`composeToolbarSlots()\` adds global/domain slots but does not remove donor-specific groups. \`WorkspaceFoundation.bind()\` then projects/intercepts donor mode/save controls for every \`DONOR\_FREE\_WORKSPACE\_HOST\`. CSS hides donor toolbar groups only for a bounded subset (\`visualize\`, \`runs\`, \`enterprise\`, plus non-product \`golden\`), producing consumer-specific suppression rather than a correctly extracted family-neutral toolbar grammar.
















This is contrary to \`OWNER-20260910-011\`, \`OWNER-20260910-013\`, \`OD-20260914-004\`, \`OD-20260918-055\`, and especially \`OD-20260920-059\`: Library is a donor/consumer, not a universal semantic/action template; toolbar reuse is grammar \+ explicitly classified universal actions \+ Surface-specific domain actions. The authority was already correct; the defect is primarily shared implementation/extraction, secondarily Writer/Controller falsification coverage.
















A related latent leak exists in \`WorkspaceHostKernel\`: \`state.surface.mode\` defaults to \`read\` for every family and \`createFamilyWorkspaceBinding()\` forwards \`mode\` into command context even when a Surface has no Read/Edit domain concept. This must be adjudicated with the toolbar correction so presentation cleanup does not leave semantic mode contamination behind.

This is a direct Owner runtime/interaction/performance rejection, not a Presentation-only complaint.  
\- Owner Firefox console evidence from the same test session records failed loopback calls to \`http://127.0.0.1:4174\`: \`/v1/persistence/bootstrap\`, \`/v1/capabilities\`, \`/v1/persistence/autosave\`, \`/v1/persistence/recovery\`, and repeated \`/v1/platform/input-direction\`; it also reports synchronous XMLHttpRequest on the main thread as detrimental to UX. The captured log contains 97 \`/v1/platform/input-direction\` failure occurrences in 107 lines.  
\- Direct Controller source inspection of exact current GitHub main establishes:  
  \- \`dist/index.html\` is a fully rendered donor/Library-style static workbench (131035 bytes) and loads \`main.js\` only after this full static shell is already available to paint.  
  \- \`stack/native-typescript/main.ts\` awaits structured \`persistenceClient.bootstrap(...)\` before mounting \`mountAcceptedRuntime(...)\` / \`mountWorkspaceHost(...)\`; \`LocalPersistenceClient\` has a 5000ms request timeout. When the local runtime transport is unavailable/misbound, the pre-rendered shell can remain visible before the current runtime composition mounts.  
  \- \`GlobalShellNavigationOwner.render()\` rewrites the adopted shell host through \`this.host.innerHTML=...\` after runtime initialization, directly explaining a visible navigation/shell swap after first paint.  
  \- \`GlobalShellNavigationOwner.navigate()\` uses \`location.assign(this.destinationURL(destination))\`, causing full-document reloads between Product destinations instead of an in-document route transition. Each navigation therefore replays static HTML parse/paint, main module bootstrap, persistence/platform probes, shell rewrite and Surface composition.  
  \- \`createLocalRuntimePlatformInputDirectionBridge()\` uses synchronous \`XMLHttpRequest\` with \`async=false\` to \`/v1/platform/input-direction\`; \`InputDirectionResolver.resolve()\` invokes bridge reads when persisted direction does not win. This creates a main-thread blocking route consistent with the observed request storm.  
  \- \`LocalRuntimePlatformWindowBridge\` also uses synchronous XMLHttpRequest for \`/v1/capabilities\` and native-window operations, creating an additional main-thread-blocking capability path.  
  \- current \`stack/local-runtime/server.mjs\` emits origin-reflecting CORS response headers and serves the observed endpoints when running. Therefore Firefox \`CORS request did not succeed / status null\` does NOT yet prove a CORS-header defect; runtime process/listener/port/origin/network truth must be directly checked on the Owner machine before root-cause classification.

\#\#\# High-value correction lessons that must survive succession
















\- Canonical finding IDs have semantic identity. Reusing the same \`Axx-PF-xxx\` string for a different issue does not close the original governed finding.  
\- Tests that call Domain methods directly can bypass Product command reachability; Product lifecycle closure requires the normal Product command/composition route where applicable.  
\- A harness/dashboard/screenshot can prove bounded mechanics but cannot substitute for normal-route provider/data truth or full state/viewport/reference parity.  
\- Truthful empty/provider-unavailable Product state cannot prove parity against a populated reference; use only governed test-only Presentation probes without promoting fixture data to Product truth, or classify parity blocked.  
\- \`demoInitial()\` or fixture defaults in normal constructors/compositions can convert synthetic data into false Product truth. Normal Product defaults must remain truthful empty/unavailable unless an admitted provider/source is bound.  
\- Strict DI must be proven at the top/group composition actually used by Product. Child strict-DI tests do not close a group-level fallback such as \`new SemanticCommandBus()\` or \`new AnalyticalCompareOwner()\`.  
\- Reviewer \`PASS/CLEAN\`, green tests, screenshots, commits and push presence never create acceptance. A blocked evidence comparison cannot later become CLEAN without a new valid evidence chain.  
\- Preserve good corrections. Do not revert whole checkpoints simply because later defects were found; repair the narrow root cause and rerun affected/regression proofs.

\#\#\# Universal visual-impact lifecycle trigger
















\`CONTROLLER\_GOVERNANCE.md\` is hardened so the CEP visual lifecycle is no longer triggered only by missions explicitly named “Visual”. Any Product mutation with a plausible user-visible effect—including frontend/UI/design/CSS, Surface composition, layout/geometry, visible data projection/state, controls/copy, responsive/RTL/Bidi behavior, focus/keyboard/pointer interaction, pane/transient/dialog behavior, accessibility Presentation or a shared owner capable of changing those outcomes—is automatically \`VISUAL\_IMPACTING\_MISSION\`. Such work must carry the applicable governed reference/profile/oracle bindings, source-bound before/after captures, region/component/state comparison, actual image inspection, interaction/accessibility/data corroboration, falsification/regression and final representative evidence before closure. An exemption requires explicit \`PROVEN\_NON\_UI\_IMPACT\` evidence; “small”, “backend”, “refactor” or “test-only” labels are not sufficient by themselves.

One central/family seed mechanism should serve compatible consumers rather than per-Surface hardcoded visual fixtures.
